30/09/24, 14:28 - Mahek: #include <iostream>
#include <string>
using namespace std;

typedef string item_type;

class list {
      class node { public:
          item_type val;
          node * next;
      };
 public :
     node * head;
     list() { head = 0;}

     bool isempty ()
     { if ( head == 0 ) return true; else return false;}

     int length ()
     { node * p = head;
       int count = 0;
       while ( p != 0 ) { count ++; p = p->next; };
       return count;
     }

     list insert ( item_type elm )
     { node * p = new node;
       if ( p == 0 )
          cerr << " fatal error : no space on heap " << endl;
          p->val = elm;
          p->next = head;
          head = p;
          return (*this);
     }

     void operator= ( node* p) { head = p; return;}

     friend ostream & operator<< (ostream &x, list l)
     { node * p = l.head;
       if (l.isempty()) {x << " Empty list " << endl; return x;}
       for ( int i = 1 ; i <= l.length(); i++ )
       { x << p->val << " "; p = p->next;
         if(i%10 == 0) x << endl;
30/09/24, 14:32 - Mahek: <Media omitted>
30/09/24, 15:39 - Mahek: #include <iostream>

template <typename T>
class Node {
public:
    T data;
    Node* next;

    Node(T val) : data(val), next(nullptr) {}
};

template <typename T>
class List {
private:
    Node<T>* head;

public:
    List() : head(nullptr) {}

    // Destructor to free the memory
    ~List() {
        while (head) {
            Node<T>* temp = head;
            head = head->next;
            delete temp;
        }
    }

    // Function to add an element to the front of the list
    void add(T val) {
        Node<T>* newNode = new Node<T>(val);
        newNode->next = head;
        head = newNode;
    }

    // Function to remove the first occurrence of the element
    void remove(T elm) {
        if (!head) {
            std::cout << "List is empty." << std::endl;
            return;
        }

        // If the element to be removed is the head
        if (head->data == elm) {
            Node<T>* temp = head;
            head = head->next;
            delete temp;
            return;
        }

        // Traverse to find the element and its previous node
        Node<T>* current = head;
        Node<T>* prev = nullptr;

        while (current && current->data != elm) {
            prev = current;
            current = current->next;
        }

        // Element not found
        if (!current) {
            std::cout << "Element not found in the list." << std::endl;
            return;
        }

        // Element found, remove it
        prev->next = current->next;
        delete current;
    }

    // Function to display the list
    void display() const {
        Node<T>* temp = head;
        while (temp) {
            std::cout << temp->data << " -> ";
            temp = temp->next;
        }
        std::cout << "NULL" << std::endl;
    }

    // Function to check if the list is empty
    bool isEmpty() const {
        return head == nullptr;
    }
};

13/11/24, 15:29 - Mahek: AWT (Abstract Window Toolkit) in Java is a part of the Java Foundation Classes (JFC) and provides a set of APIs for creating and managing graphical user interface (GUI) components. It’s one of the original GUI toolkits provided by Java, allowing developers to build windows-based applications with various user interface components, such as buttons, text fields, labels, and more. AWT provides a simple, platform-independent way to create graphical interfaces, though it relies on the underlying platform's native GUI components, which can lead to differences in appearance and behavior across operating systems. Key Components of AWT
Here’s an overview of the main AWT components and containers:

1. Containers
Containers are components that hold other components (including other containers). The main types of containers in AWT include:

Frame: Represents the main window of an application. It’s a top-level window with a title, close button, minimize button, and maximize button.
Panel: A generic container used to group other components within a window. It’s often used as a sub-container within a frame.
Window: A top-level container used to create standalone pop-up windows without title bars or borders.
Dialog: A pop-up window used for interactions with the user, typically used to gather input or provide information. A dialog can be modal (blocking other windows) or non-modal.
2. Components
AWT provides various UI components, which can be placed within containers. Some of the key components include:

Button: A clickable button, typically used to initiate an action or event.
Label: Displays a non-editable text label. Useful for displaying information or labels for other components.
TextField: A single-line text input field, where users can enter or modify text.
TextArea: A multi-line text input area, allowing for larger blocks of text.
Checkbox: Represents a checkbox that can be checked or unchecked, often used in forms.
CheckboxGroup: Groups checkboxes together so that only one can be selected at a time, similar to radio buttons.
Choice: A dropdown list of options, allowing users to select one from a list.
List: Displays a list of options, with the option for users to select multiple items.
Scrollbar: Used for horizontal or vertical scrolling, commonly used for navigation within a larger interface.
3. Layouts
Layout managers in AWT control the arrangement of components within containers. Some commonly used layout managers are:

FlowLayout: Arranges components in a left-to-right flow, wrapping to the next line when necessary.
BorderLayout: Divides the container into five regions: North, South, East, West, and Center, placing one component in each region.
GridLayout: Arranges components in a grid with a specified number of rows and columns, making all components the same size.
GridBagLayout: A more flexible layout manager that allows components of different sizes and positions, often used for complex interfaces.
4. Event Handling
AWT supports event-driven programming, meaning it can respond to user actions such as button clicks, mouse movements, and keyboard input. Events are handled using Event Listeners, and some of the commonly used listeners are:

ActionListener: Handles action events, commonly used with buttons.
MouseListener: Handles mouse events such as clicks, enters, exits, and releases.
KeyListener: Manages keyboard events like key presses and releases.
WindowListener: Manages window events like opening, closing, and minimizing.
Summary
AWT components provide the basic building blocks for Java GUIs and are useful for simple interfaces. However, AWT is considered more limited and outdated compared to Swing, which offers more powerful, flexible, and customizable components for creating modern Java applications.






You said:
write an easy JAVA GUI program to Add two numbers using AWT
ChatGPT said:
Here’s a simple Java GUI program using AWT to add two numbers. The program creates a small window where the user can enter two numbers, click a button to add them, and display the result.

import java.awt.*;
import java.awt.event.*;

public class AddTwoNumbers extends Frame implements ActionListener {
    // Declaring components
    Label label1, label2, labelResult;
    TextField textField1, textField2, textFieldResult;
    Button addButton;

    public AddTwoNumbers() {
        // Setting up the frame
        setTitle("Add Two Numbers");
        setSize(300, 200);
        setLayout(new FlowLayout());

        // Initializing components
        label1 = new Label("Number 1:");
        textField1 = new TextField(10);

        label2 = new Label("Number 2:");
        textField2 = new TextField(10);

        addButton = new Button("Add");
        addButton.addActionListener(this);  // Add ActionListener to the button

        labelResult = new Label("Result:");
        textFieldResult = new TextField(10);
        textFieldResult.setEditable(false);  // Make result field read-only

        // Adding components to the frame
        add(label1);
        add(textField1);
        add(label2);
        add(textField2);
        add(addButton);
        add(labelResult);
        add(textFieldResult);

        // Setting up frame to close on click of close button
        addWindowListener(new WindowAdapter() {
            public void windowClosing(WindowEvent we) {
                dispose();
            }
        });

        setVisible(true);
    }

    // This method is called when the button is clicked
    public void actionPerformed(ActionEvent e) {
        try {
            // Getting numbers from text fields and parsing them as integers
            int num1 = Integer.parseInt(textField1.getText());
            int num2 = Integer.parseInt(textField2.getText());

            // Calculating the result and displaying it
            int result = num1 + num2;
            textFieldResult.setText(String.valueOf(result));
        } catch (NumberFormatException ex) {
            textFieldResult.setText("Invalid input");
        }
    }

    public static void main(String[] args) {
        new AddTwoNumbers();  // Creating an instance of the frame
    }
}
Explanation of the Code
Frame and Layout: We use Frame to create the main window and FlowLayout to arrange components in a sequence.
Components:
Label is used to show the text prompts.
TextField is used for input and displaying the result.
Button is used to trigger the addition.
Action Listener: When the "Add" button is clicked, actionPerformed() method is called, which retrieves and adds the two numbers.
Error Handling: If the input is invalid, it displays "Invalid input" in the result field.
Window Closing: The WindowAdapter is used to close the frame when the user clicks the close button.
How to Run
Save the code to a file named AddTwoNumbers.java.
Compile using javac AddTwoNumbers.java.
Run using java AddTwoNumbers.
This program provides a simple addition tool with a GUI in AWT!

can u make the code simpler 

3/3

ChatGPT said:
Sure! Here’s a simplified version of the Java AWT program to add two numbers. This version removes some of the extra comments and keeps the code straightforward.

import java.awt.*;
import java.awt.event.*;

public class AddTwoNumbers extends Frame implements ActionListener {
    TextField num1Field, num2Field, resultField;
    Button addButton;

    public AddTwoNumbers() {
        // Frame setup
        setLayout(new FlowLayout());
        setSize(250, 150);
        setTitle("Add Two Numbers");

        // Components
        num1Field = new TextField(10);
        num2Field = new TextField(10);
        resultField = new TextField(10);
        resultField.setEditable(false);

        addButton = new Button("Add");
        addButton.addActionListener(this);

        // Adding components to the frame
        add(new Label("Number 1:"));
        add(num1Field);
        add(new Label("Number 2:"));
        add(num2Field);
        add(addButton);
        add(new Label("Result:"));
        add(resultField);

        // Close button functionality
        addWindowListener(new WindowAdapter() {
            public void windowClosing(WindowEvent we) {
                dispose();
            }
        });

        setVisible(true);
    }

    public void actionPerformed(ActionEvent e) {
        try {
            int num1 = Integer.parseInt(num1Field.getText());
            int num2 = Integer.parseInt(num2Field.getText());
            resultField.setText(String.valueOf(num1 + num2));
        } catch (NumberFormatException ex) {
            resultField.setText("Invalid input");
        }
    }

    public static void main(String[] args) {
        new AddTwoNumbers();
    }
}
Key Changes
Compact Layout: The code is structured without additional comments, focusing on clarity and functionality.
Labels and TextFields: Labels, input fields, and result fields are all added directly in sequence.
Minimal Error Handling: If input is invalid, the result field shows "Invalid input".
How to Run
Save the code to a file named AddTwoNumbers.java.
Compile it using javac AddTwoNumbers.java.
Run it with java AddTwoNumbers.
This simpler version achieves the same functionality with a clean, compact layout.

26/03/25, 10:49 - Priyanshu: #include <iostream>
#include <string>
using namespace std;

string greedyLCS(const string &X, const string &Y) {
    int m = X.length(), n = Y.length();
    int i = 0, j = 0;
    string lcs = "";

    while (i < m && j < n) {
        if (X[i] == Y[j]) {
            lcs += X[i];
            i++;
            j++;
        } else {
            if (X.substr(i).find(Y[j]) != string::npos) {
                i++;
            } else {
                j++;
            }
        }
    }
    return lcs;
}

int main() {
    string X = "ACDBE";
    string Y = "ABCDE";

    cout << "Greedy LCS: " << greedyLCS(X, Y) << endl;

    return 0;
}
26/03/25, 10:50 - Priyanshu: #include <iostream>
#include <vector>
#include <string>
using namespace std;

string findLCS(string X, string Y) {
    int m = X.length(), n = Y.length();
    vector<vector<int>> dp(m + 1, vector<int>(n + 1, 0));

    for (int i = 1; i <= m; i++) {
        for (int j = 1; j <= n; j++) {
            if (X[i - 1] == Y[j - 1]) {
                dp[i][j] = 1 + dp[i - 1][j - 1];
            } else {
                dp[i][j] = max(dp[i - 1][j], dp[i][j - 1]);
            }
        }
    }

    int i = m, j = n;
    string lcs = "";
    
    while (i > 0 && j > 0) {
        if (X[i - 1] == Y[j - 1]) {
            lcs = X[i - 1] + lcs;
            i--;
            j--;
        } else if (dp[i - 1][j] > dp[i][j - 1]) {
            i--;
        } else {
            j--;
        }
    }

    return lcs;
}

int main() {
    string X = "ACDBE";
    string Y = "ABCDE";

    cout << "LCS using DP: " << findLCS(X, Y) << endl;

    return 0;
}
26/03/25, 10:51 - Mahek: #include <iostream>
#include <vector>
using namespace std;

// Function to compute LCS using Dynamic Programming
string dpLCS(string X, string Y) {
    int m = X.length(), n = Y.length();
    vector<vector<int>> dp(m + 1, vector<int>(n + 1, 0));

    // Build the DP table
    for (int i = 1; i <= m; i++) {
        for (int j = 1; j <= n; j++) {
            if (X[i - 1] == Y[j - 1])
                dp[i][j] = dp[i - 1][j - 1] + 1;
            else
                dp[i][j] = max(dp[i - 1][j], dp[i][j - 1]);
        }
    }

    // Backtracking to find the LCS string
    string lcs = "";
    int i = m, j = n;
    while (i > 0 && j > 0) {
        if (X[i - 1] == Y[j - 1]) {
            lcs = X[i - 1] + lcs;
            i--; j--;
        } else if (dp[i - 1][j] > dp[i][j - 1]) {
            i--;
        } else {
            j--;
        }
    }
    return lcs;
}

int main() {
    string X = "ABCDEF", Y = "ACEBDF";
    cout << "DP LCS: " << dpLCS(X, Y) << endl;
    return 0;
}
